# Executive Summary

**The Technology Operating Picture**

The global technology landscape is undergoing a fundamental transition from conversational AI to **autonomous agentic systems**. Over the past year, the industry's focus has shifted from building larger chatbots to deploying software agents capable of independently executing complex, multi-step tasks within production environments. This shift is fundamentally altering the economics of software development, infrastructure design, and system maintenance, creating a new set of operational bottlenecks that directly impact the daily work of IT professionals.

As these autonomous agents are integrated into enterprise workflows, they are exposing severe limitations in current infrastructure. On the compute side, the industry is hitting a physical wall; power grid constraints and data center capacity limits are forcing major cloud providers to seek alternative energy sources, such as nuclear power, while delaying or canceling major supercomputer projects. On the software side, the sheer volume of AI-generated code is causing **code sprawl**, clogging continuous integration (CI) pipelines, and turning traditional code review into an unsustainable bottleneck. 

For the IT professional, these shifts mean that the value of simply writing code is declining, while the demand for skills in **runtime verification, system observability, and resource orchestration** is rising. The primary challenge is no longer generating software, but verifying its safety, managing its token consumption, and ensuring that autonomous agents do not introduce silent failures or security vulnerabilities into production environments.

---

## SECTOR SHIFTS

### Hardware and Chips

The physical scaling of AI has officially collided with the limits of the global energy grid. Major cloud providers are increasingly bypassing traditional utilities to secure their own power, evidenced by Amazon and Google partnering directly with nuclear energy providers to power their next-generation data centers. This energy bottleneck is so severe that flagship supercomputer projects are being canceled or delayed solely due to grid connection backlogs. 

Simultaneously, hardware architecture is bifurcating. On one end, we are seeing massive, highly integrated data center clusters like Huawei’s Atlas superpods that utilize near-packaged optics to reduce data latency. On the other end, there is a aggressive push toward **local AI compute**. Laptops and mobile devices are shipping with dedicated neural processing units (NPUs) capable of running highly optimized local models. However, this local shift has created a new engineering challenge: the **vector indexes** required for local retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) are frequently exceeding the memory footprint of the AI models themselves, forcing hardware manufacturers to prioritize memory bandwidth over raw processing power.

*The core pattern here is that physical power availability and memory bandwidth have replaced silicon design as the primary bottlenecks for AI scaling.*

### Cloud, Infrastructure and Platforms

Cloud infrastructure is evolving to support the high-frequency request volumes generated by autonomous agents. Traditional container architectures are being challenged by **WebAssembly (Wasm)**, which is increasingly outperforming standard containers at the edge due to its near-instant startup times and lower memory overhead. This is particularly critical for AI agents that need to spin up, execute a single task, and terminate without incurring the latency of a full container lifecycle. 

At the same time, platform providers are consolidating the developer tooling ecosystem. Cloudflare’s aggressive acquisitions of serverless and runtime technologies, alongside its open-sourcing of development frameworks, indicate a clear strategy to build the economic and execution layer for agentic web traffic. Infrastructure management is also shifting; tools like Terraform are being repurposed to diagnose and recover broken cloud states in real time, while **Domain Name System (Base DNS)** management is being elevated to a core infrastructure discipline to handle the dynamic routing requirements of thousands of active software agents.

*The trend here is the optimization of the edge runtime environment around lightweight, low-latency execution models like WebAssembly.*

### AI and Data

The software industry is rapidly standardizing on the **Model Context Protocol (MCP)** to connect autonomous agents to external APIs and databases. Rather than building custom integrations for every tool, developers are using MCP to expose data sources to agents within a unified context budget. However, this has introduced an "API tax." Agents frequently stall or generate massive cloud bills because they lack infrastructure context, sometimes executing thousands of database queries or token-heavy API calls to complete a single task. 

To combat these costs, the industry is shifting from basic RAG to **GraphRAG** to solve complex, multi-hop reasoning failures. Additionally, the major model providers—including Anthropic, Google, and DeepSeek—are engaged in an aggressive price war, drastically reducing token costs and introducing features like **prompt caching** to make continuous agent execution financially viable. Despite these cost reductions, engineering teams are finding that synthetic data is not a silver bullet; low trust in the referential integrity of synthetic datasets is holding back automated model training, forcing a renewed focus on high-quality, human-curated data pipelines.

*The shift here is from model-centric scaling to agent-centric orchestration, where context management and token efficiency dictate system viability.*

### Security and Trust

The rise of coding agents has turned traditional software supply chains into a primary attack vector. Security teams are moving away from standard container images toward **distroless containers** to minimize the attack surface of OCI images. This shift is driven by the reality that autonomous agents frequently import unvetted third-party packages to solve coding tasks, introducing vulnerabilities like the **ChainDrop** supply chain worm directly into development pipelines. 

Furthermore, AI-driven vulnerability scanning has turned the age of legacy frameworks into an immediate security emergency. Legacy Java Spring and PHP applications are facing unprecedented levels of automated exploitation, as malicious agents can now scan, identify, and write exploits for unpatched vulnerabilities faster than human teams can triage them. This has forced organizations to adopt automated remediation tools, such as those provided by Chainguard and Azul, to patch Java Virtual Machines (JVMs) and package registries before they can be targeted.

*The pattern here is that AI-accelerated exploit discovery is rendering manual vulnerability triage obsolete, forcing a shift toward automated, real-time dependency patching.*

### Enterprise and Industry Software

Enterprise SaaS platforms are transitioning from static dashboards to **agent-delivered answers**. Major vendors like Workday and SAP are opening their core HR and financial data to external AI agents, allowing businesses to automate back-office operations without human intervention. This transition is forcing a complete re-evaluation of the software development lifecycle (SDLC). 

Because coding agents can generate pull requests in seconds, continuous integration (CI) pipelines have become a massive bottleneck. The sheer volume of automated code changes is causing **code sprawl**, leading to "code review theater" where senior engineers, overwhelmed by the volume of pull requests, approve changes without fully understanding their downstream architectural impacts. To mitigate this, enterprise DevOps platforms are pivoting toward AI-first strategies that use automated agents to test and verify the code written by other agents, shifting the human role from code writer to system gatekeeper.

*The core pattern is the automation of the merge-to-test workflow to prevent AI-generated code from destabilizing enterprise software architecture.*

---

## MONEY AND POWER

Capital is rapidly retreating from pure-play software-as-a-service (SaaS) startups and flowing directly into **physical infrastructure and physical AI**. Venture capital and private equity are consolidating around data center land, power generation, and robotics hardware. The financial distress appearing in the AI debt markets—where high-leverage infrastructure providers are seeing their debt trade at significant discounts—indicates that the market is correcting away from speculative software valuations toward tangible assets. 

Pricing power is concentrating in the hands of platform providers that control the execution environments for AI agents. Companies that own the developer runtime, the package registry, and the edge network (such as Cloudflare, AWS, and GitHub) are establishing themselves as the toll booths of the agentic era. Conversely, traditional database and middleware vendors are losing pricing power as features like vector search become commoditized and integrated directly into standard data platforms.

---

## WHAT THIS MEANS FOR SINGAPORE AND SOUTHEAST ASIA

For IT professionals in Singapore and the wider Southeast Asian region, this global shift will manifest as a surge in demand for **hybrid infrastructure engineers** who can manage localized, sovereign AI deployments. As Singapore establishes itself as a global hub for AI safety and model evaluation through partnerships with firms like Anthropic, local organizations will face intense regulatory pressure to audit and verify the decisions made by autonomous systems. 

Furthermore, the regional push for localized LLMs, such as **SEA-LION** and **Sahabat-AI**, means that local engineering teams must become proficient in managing mixed AI stacks that combine American frontier models with regional, open-weight alternatives. IT professionals who can bridge the gap between cloud-native orchestration (Kubernetes, WebAssembly) and local compliance standards will find themselves at the center of the region's hiring demand.

- China’s expanding carbon market may increase demand for overseas credits due to a potential shortfall in domestic offsets.

- Former Chinese broadcasting regulator official Yang Xiaowei was sentenced to 12 years for bribery involving state telecommunications companies.

- China tightened export controls on drone motors, controllers, and blades while easing restrictions on agricultural drones.

- The U.S. Federal Communications Commission expanded restrictions on electronics using Huawei and ZTE parts, extending to certain chips and modules.

- The FCC proposed a rule that could end the recognition of Chinese mainland electronics testing facilities' credentials by 2028.

- A U.S. trade investigation is examining whether power systems used in high-performance computing by firms like Luxshare and Foxconn infringe on a Vicor patent.

- Evergrande took full control of NEVS after entrepreneur Kai Johan Jiang struggled to make his EV business viable.

- A Huawei auto spinoff partnered with CATL to develop automated battery swapping technology.

- The Iran conflict is accelerating Dubai's tech sector shift and deepening ties with China.

- China's factory utilization increased slightly while the auto sector experienced a slump.

<summary><b>Model Scope</b></summary>


**AI**


- DeepSeek released DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, a 552B parameter multimodal MoE model featuring KV cache compression and 1M token context support.

- NeoHorse-1 introduced a family of agent-native models designed for recursive self-improvement via agentic post-training and intelligent routing.

- Prompteina was developed as an all-atom protein structure generation model that supports design objectives specified in natural language.

- Researchers proposed CNF-MCE, a tokenizer training approach and encoding algorithm that improves token efficiency and compression rates for large language models.

- SparkDiffusion was introduced as a unified acceleration framework for video diffusion models, achieving significant speedups on single-GPU setups like RTX 5090 and H100.

- A new evolutionary framework uses LLMs to discover state-of-the-art quantum circuits, reducing Toffoli and qubit counts across various computational tasks.

- The MiMo-V2.6 model series was introduced, focusing on scaling reinforcement learning compute and infrastructure for model self-improvement.

- Qwen3.8-Omni-Flash was released as a natively multimodal agentic model supporting long-context reasoning and real-time interaction via the Qwen-Live-Harness framework.

- Artisan was introduced as a multi-agent pipeline for physically grounded 3D asset generation, incorporating physical simulation feedback.

- WOVEN was introduced as a training source and benchmark for visual transition reasoning to improve spatial and physical reasoning in multimodal large language models.

- ReplaySSM was introduced to optimize state-space model (SSM) inference by unrolling recurrence and caching inputs, delivering throughput improvements for models like Mamba-2.

- Researchers highlighted limitations in AI autoformalisation, noting that OpenAI's announced Navier-Stokes proof contains mismatches between the natural language proof and the Lean verification.

- VideoJev was introduced as a family of models that treat video understanding and interaction as structured decision tasks, improving inference speed and temporal grounding.

- The Humanize Olympiad Agent (HOA) framework was introduced, coordinating multiple agent sessions with feedback channels to improve performance on scientific and olympiad tasks.

- AlphaNIST was introduced as a reinforcement learning framework that enables robots to discover assembly strategies on the NIST Assembly Task Board #1 using sparse binary rewards.

- Shanghai Academy of Artificial Intelligence for Science (SAIS), ModelScope, and Datawhale co-developed the "AI4S in Action" course.

- NVIDIA open-sourced Nemotron 3.5 Super VL, a 120B MoE multi-modal agent model with 12B active parameters and 1M token context support.

- Meta open-sourced Muse-Spark-Open, an 816B MoE multi-modal agent model with 42B active parameters and 1M context support.

- ModelScope community reported 8,577 new models, 1,017 new datasets, and 717 new applications added between September 18 and October 10.

- ModelScope launched the "Mule Agent Builder" to enable the construction of agents using a "Base Agent + Skills + Knowledge" paradigm.

- Alipay launched a "Payment Integration Skill" on the ModelScope Skills Center, allowing developers to integrate payment functionality using natural language.

- Zhongzhi FlagOS released "FlagOS Skills 1.0," an AI Agent skill library for heterogeneous AI chips, supporting model migration and performance optimization.

- ChatPPT and ModelScope launched ChatPPT MCP 2.0, a cloud-based intelligent agent service.

- The ModelScope community launched the "Skills Center" to provide a platform for combining open-source models with specific functional skills.

- OneScience launched "OneSkills," a library of科研 (scientific research) agent skills, on the ModelScope community platform.

- Flowy company's Herdsman local model inference platform integrated support for Qwen3.8-27B and Intel hardware.

- ModelScope DiffSynth team open-sourced Z-Image-Turbo-DistillPatch, a LoRA training solution for maintaining image generation acceleration.

- The ModelScope community launched the "ModelScope Skills Hub" to allow developers to explore and combine open-source models with functional skills.

- The "AI4S in Action" course was launched by SAIS, ModelScope, and Datawhale to focus on AI for Science applications.

- ModelScope community introduced the "Monthly Influential Authors Ranking" to recognize contributions in open-source models and creative works.

- The "Agent Skills Technical Protocol" was published to standardize the development and implementation of domain-specific agent capabilities.

- A new "FileGovernor" skill was released for local AI agents to perform disk cleanup and document management with 100% local inference.

- The "AI PC Agent Skills" project introduced a system for student grade analysis and visualization using local LLMs (≤35B).

- ModelScope community launched the "MCP Hub" for deploying and sharing Model Context Protocol (MCP) tools.



**HARDWARE**

<details markdown="1">
<summary><b>LilLog</b></summary>


**AI**


- Recursive self-improvement in AI models, where systems improve their own cognitive machinery, is being explored as a feedback loop for model development.

- Frontier labs like Anthropic and OpenAI are experiencing drastically accelerated research development speeds.

- Scaling laws in deep learning demonstrate that training loss decreases predictably as model size, dataset size, and compute are scaled up.

- Test-time compute and Chain-of-thought prompting are being utilized to significantly improve model performance.

- Reward hacking in reinforcement learning (RL) remains a critical challenge for the deployment of autonomous AI models, particularly as RLHF becomes standard for alignment.

- Extrinsic hallucination in large language models, where outputs are not grounded in pre-training data or world knowledge, remains a major hurdle for factual reliability.

- Research is shifting toward video generation using diffusion models, which requires solving for temporal consistency and high-quality video data collection.

- High-quality data remains a critical bottleneck for deep learning, with a noted industry trend of prioritizing model architecture work over data collection and curation.

- Adversarial attacks and jailbreak prompts pose significant security risks to large language models, complicating the deployment of safe, aligned AI systems.

- Autonomous agent systems powered by LLMs are emerging, utilizing planning, memory, and tool use to function as general problem solvers.

- Prompt engineering is being utilized as an empirical method to steer LLM behavior and alignment without updating model weights.
